= SPEC_LOG — Discrepancy, Fit and Correction Log
:sectnums:
:sectnumlevels: 5
:toc:
:toclevels: 4

This is the working record behind `SPEC.adoc`. It holds everything `SPEC.adoc`
deliberately does not: provenance, measurement method, superseded readings,
fitted constants, and the discrepancies between what the driver's own code
implies and what the reference renders actually do.

Entries are sorted by date, latest first. Every correction to `SPEC.adoc` is
recorded here together with the evidence that forced it; `SPEC.adoc` itself
carries only the corrected result.

Be wary that an apparent discrepancy is sometimes not a defect in `SPEC.adoc` at
all, but a mechanism `SPEC.adoc` documents and this project has not implemented.
Check before treating a "the spec is silent" claim in this document as fact.

Two numbering series run through the entries and are referred to by number
throughout: **items** (`item1`…`item58`) are discrepancies and gaps between
`SPEC.adoc` and this project's engine; **entries** (`entry1`…`entry17`) are
fitted values, shipped or rejected. The numbers are stable identifiers, not
positions in this document — the entries are ordered by date, not by number.

== Conventions

=== What `[F:fitted]` means

A value tagged `[F:fitted]` in this project's source or docs was tuned
**empirically against reference audio renders** (probe MIDI files vs. their
captured reference audio in `probe-results/`), not recovered from the
original binary's disassembly. It exists because the real consumption code
for that value is `[O]` (outside every disassembled/examined range in
`SPEC.adoc`) -- empirical fitting against reference audio is the sanctioned
fallback for exactly that situation, but it must always be visibly flagged
as a fit, never presented or graded as `[A:recovered]`.

Rules for anything tagged `[F:fitted]`:

* It must cite the probe/reference it was fit against and the exact error
  metric used.
* It must cite where a future RE pass should look to recover the real
  formula (a `SPEC.adoc` pointer, an address, a function), so the fit can be
  retired once real recovery happens.
* It must never be cited as evidence of what the original binary does --
  only as an evidence-based placeholder standing in for it.
* If a fit is measured and found to make things *worse* against the
  project's own regression harness, it is not shipped, but the attempt and
  its measurements are still recorded here (see entry 1) -- an honestly
  reported negative result is not a discarded one.

'''

=== The verification ceiling

Overstating what the reference captures can prove is the single worst failure
mode available to this project. **No stage of it may report bit-exactness as
verified** — not `SPEC.adoc`, not this log, not any test-harness output.

**Capture provenance.** The references were produced by playing MIDI back on
an emulated Windows XP guest and capturing the resulting audio through a
host-side "Stereo Mix"-style loopback device, not by extracting samples from
the driver's own output buffer. That chain is itself a source of noise and
timing artifacts distinct from anything `swmidi.sys` does, and everything
below follows from it.

**Noise floor.** True-silence padding across the probe recordings is confined
to `{-2..2}` LSB, ≈99.98% within `{-1,0,1}`, with near-zero left/right
cross-channel correlation (≈0.001) — a small, near-independent, roughly
±1 LSB dither floor per channel, not signal `[M]`. Lag-1 autocorrelation on
the same quiet regions measures 0.04–0.31 per file (pooled ≈0.135) `[M]`; the
qualitative characterization holds, but no tighter numeric autocorrelation
bound should be repeated as a verified ceiling. **Because of this floor,
bit-exactness cannot be empirically proven with these captures.**
Sample-exactness remains the design goal.

**Repeatability differs by material type.** Reference renders of the same
event repeat to approximately **1% on percussive attacks** and **0.02% on
sustained tones** `[M]`. A single tolerance applied to both will either pass
bad percussive output or fail good sustained output.

**Capture-chain timing offset — negative, file-dependent, not a property of
the synth.** Measured onset-alignment offsets between a probe manifest's
declared MIDI event time and the corresponding audible onset are **−0.688 s**
(probe 20: declared 1.200 s, measured 0.512 s) and **−0.802 s** (probe 18)
`[M]`. The magnitude is stable within a single probe file's own event train
but differs materially between files, which is itself the evidence that this
is a per-capture-session artifact — differing lead-in silence, or a differing
capture-clock zero point — not a property of `swmidi.sys`. **Always recover
alignment by cross-correlation against the actual audio; never assume a fixed
offset**, and never use either figure to calibrate timing inside the engine.

**The 22050→44100 upsampling stage is a hard ceiling on any 44.1 kHz
comparison.** Zero-order hold and linear interpolation are both eliminated
(0.23% and 0.87% of odd-indexed samples match) `[M]`, and `SPEC.adoc`
xref:SPEC.adoc#s6-11[§6.11] recovers the real stage's algorithm class from
`kmixer.sys` itself — but its tap coefficients remain `[O]`. The only
ceiling-free comparison is at 22050 Hz.

**Per-reference provenance differs, and this bounds how much of each one's
residual is attributable to the engine rather than to how the file was
captured.**

* `field/corridor.flac` was captured over the same loopback path but **with a
  DAW application left open** on the capturing machine. The user who produced
  it reports it sounds measurably brighter than the other two. A DAW held
  open plausibly forced the guest or host device to a different operating
  rate (e.g. 48000 Hz), moving the upsampling stage away from the clean 2×
  case and/or adding host-side processing to the loopback path. This was not
  confirmed spectrally — the three references are three different pieces of
  music, so content differences dominate any spectral-tilt comparison, and no
  matched-content A/B exists. **Recorded as user-reported capture provenance,
  not as a measured value.** Net effect: an unknown, plausibly
  larger-than-dither share of this file's residual is capture-path artifact,
  so its exact spectral match is not a precision target.
* `field/Kot_and_A64-GENERAL_SERUM.flac` was captured over the same path with
  **no DAW open**, deliberately un-normalized so it clips naturally at the
  int16 rail — 1.06% of samples sit at `+32767`/`-32768` `[M]` (a separately
  reported 1.07% for the same file differs only by measurement-tool
  rounding). The cleanest of the three; treated as the acceptance reference.
* `field/Strobe-faffaeefafaefae.flac` was also captured with no DAW open.
  Also clean; treated as the aliasing-model acid test.

All three sit above the ±1 LSB dither floor. GENERAL SERUM and faffaee are
weighted **above** corridor as fidelity targets.

None of this weakens the findings already recorded about the engine's own
render behaviour against `field/corridor.mid` — the release-time bug that
left it truncated (~70 s to 82.8 s across snapshots, never latching finished,
against a ~52.4 s reference) and its fix (52.57 s against a 52.43 s
reference, xref:#item15[item 15]), together with the onset-timing accuracy
check on the same file, concern only the engine's internal timing and
termination. They are independent of which capture path recorded the
reference, and they stand.

'''

=== Harness re-baseline, 2026-07-25

The project's Python fitting/comparison harness (a corpus-comparison driver
plus per-feature fit-check scripts) is not retained in this tree; every
mention of it below (and of its individual scripts and recorded JSON
results by name) is historical narration of a check that was run at the
time, not a pointer to a file that exists now. Its rendering role is now
filled by the native `dist/msgs-render` binary. This harness's alignment was replaced (see
`envelope_align_offset` and the two-stage alignment in `align_and_grade`). Every
`compare_spectral_22050.overall_db` figure in this file that was recorded
before this change was computed on a **misaligned** reference/render pair
for a large part of the corpus, and is therefore not comparable with any
figure recorded after it.

**What was wrong.** The old aligner cross-correlated raw waveforms over the
whole file. The probe corpus is mostly ladders of *identical repeated notes*
at fixed spacing, so the waveform correlates nearly as well one whole note-
slot off as at the true offset, and the FFT peak routinely locked onto the
wrong slot. Confirmed on the gain/pan probes, where every disagreement with
a hand-measured offset was an exact integer multiple of that probe's own
note spacing: 03_velocity off by 8.07 s, 24_gain_staging by 8.11 s,
25_pan_law by exactly two notes.

**The fix.** Two stages: a coarse pass correlating 10 ms **RMS envelopes**
(the envelope keeps the per-step *level* that distinguishes one rung of a
ladder from the next, which is exactly what waveform correlation discards),
then a sample-domain refine within ±50 ms of the coarse result, so the final
offset is still sample-accurate. Validated against seven independently
hand-measured offsets: max error **29 ms**, five of seven within 6 ms, versus
errors up to 8.11 s before. All seven `IMPLAUSIBLE_OFFSET` flags in the
corpus cleared (7 → 0); envelope correlation r = 0.86–0.98.

**Scale of the correction.** 14 of 24 graded probes were misaligned. Corpus
mean −8.025 → **−10.113 dB**; GENERAL_SERUM −2.27 → **−4.24 dB** with its
envelope correlation r 0.642 → **0.831**.

**These are measurement corrections, not synth improvements.** Not one line
of `src/engine/` changed between those two numbers. The synth was always this
close; the harness was mis-reporting it.

**What this does and does not invalidate.** Conclusions drawn from *direct
per-step measurement at a hand-recovered alignment* stand unaffected —
notably xref:entry7[Entry 7]'s per-channel pan table and the `[M: probe 07]` settlements
in `SPEC_LOG` §4 and §9, all of which used RMS-envelope alignment
precisely because the harness number was visibly untrustworthy. What is
invalidated is any *corpus-wide comparison table* computed by the old
harness, including xref:entry7[Entry 7] §4 (re-measured — see the note there) and the
before/after tables in Entries 1–6. Those tables are kept as recorded; they
are history, not current measurements.

'''

'''

[#item58]
== 2026-08-03 — Three findings dropped by the comment-reduction pass without being moved anywhere: EG2 shape corroboration, a Branch B cadence data point, and the `rt_pow` worst-case check — item58

Recovered verbatim from source comments that were compacted or removed during
the comment-reduction pass, before this document existed to hold them. None
of these change a shipped value; they are provenance that would otherwise
have been lost. Grouped here because all three were found the same way
(diffing decimal literals that left `src/` against what survives in the
docs), not because they are related to each other.

=== EG2 segment shape: probe 34 bank 2 corroboration for xref:#item20[item 20]'s linear-in-cents shape

Migrated from a `src/engine/voice.c` comment on `EG2_LINEAR_SEGMENTS`. Not a
new decision -- xref:#item20[item 20] already ships linear-in-cents segments
and cites the -2.73 dB / -2.59 dB corpus improvement that decided it. This is
the shape-corroboration reasoning behind that decision, which never made it
into the log.

EG1's segments are exponential in amplitude because that is linear in dB --
the unit its destination (level) actually lives in. EG2's destination is
PITCH, whose natural unit is cents, so the same DLS-units convention makes
EG2's segments linear in CENTS instead, i.e. linear in the envelope level.
Rendering EG2 with EG1's exponential coefficient produces a visibly "eased"
sweep where the reference's is straight -- spotted by eye on probe 34 bank 2.

That visual read was then weakly corroborated, not proven, numerically:
linear-in-cents R² 0.5403 against exponential R² 0.5161. Both are poor fits
-- probe 34 is a tire-screech SFX, hostile to pitch tracking -- so the shape
claim rests on the visual and on the DLS units convention, not on that
margin. `SPEC.adoc` xref:SPEC.adoc#s6-6[S6.6] marks the envelope generator's
own behaviour `[O]`. `EG2_LINEAR_SEGMENTS` stays overridable to compare the
two renderings.

=== Branch B reserve top-up cadence sweep: a fourth data point, still OPEN

Migrated from a `src/engine/voice.c` comment on the same VMA chain
xref:#item21-reap[item 21's reap addendum] recovers
(`[A:0x12be7]`/`0x12bd6`/`0x13054`/`[A:0x130af]`/`[A:0x184c0]`) --
`TopUpReserve` runs at `[A:0x12be7]`, once per `0x12bd6`, which runs once per
`0x13054` `[A:0x130af]`, which renders one KS buffer, so the driver's true
call cadence is once per service block; the driver allocates 1024-frame
buffers `[A:0x184c0]`.

Wired up at that cadence, this model breaks the very probe S5.5 bounds it
with. Measured, corpus mean / `20_voice_count` residual:

[options="header"]
|===
| configuration | corpus mean residual | `20_voice_count` residual
| 256 frames (our block) | -31.0087 | -17.25
| 1024 frames (the driver's buffer) | -31.1163 | -22.95
| this value (shipped) | -31.1440 | -24.14
| pre-segment build | -31.1483 | -24.13
|===

So the recovered cadence is not usable AS a cadence, which means something
else in Branch B is wrong. The suspect named in the comment: `need`. A voice
Branch B marks stays `active` while it drains, so it never reduces
`reserve_free`, and the next call recomputes the same shortfall and marks
another batch. The driver's own `reserveCount` may be incremented at MARK
time rather than at recycle time, which would make one -- the comment is cut
off there in the source and does not say what "one" refers to.

**This is OPEN, not retired.** xref:#item21-reap[Item 21's reap addendum]
examined the same VMA chain earlier and reached a different diagnosis for
its own two-way sweep (`FAST_RELEASE_S` clamped 5x too slow, not `need`);
the two are not reconciled here, only both recorded as found.

=== `rt_pow` worst-case precision check against SPEC.adoc S3.4.1

Migrated from a `src/unit.c` comment; the check itself still runs, compacted.
`SPEC.adoc` xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-4-1[S3.4.1]'s own documented worst case -- a
real, checkable number, not an invented tolerance -- is `tc =
4330.571090698242` evaluated as `rt_pow(2.0, tc/1200.0) * 22050.0`, which
must truncate to 269009 samples.

Executed check: libm gives 269009.99992038216 -- exactly SPEC.adoc's own
printed figure -- and this project's `rt_pow` gives 269009.99992788181; both
truncate to 269009.

'''

[#item51]
== 2026-08-03 — Bank/Program note-lookahead within a MIDI tick is an empirical fit to probes 38/40/42, not the driver's own reach-to-end mechanism — item51, SHIPPED (`SMF_BANKPROG_LOOKAHEAD`)

**Status: `[F:fitted]`, empirical fit against captures, contradicts a confirmed `[A]` reading of the disassembly.** Migrated from a `src/engine/smf.c` comment (the largest single comment block in the engine, `slide_notes_past_bankprog()`/`SMF_BANKPROG_LOOKAHEAD`).

`SPEC.adoc` xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-2-1[S4.2.1]/xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-7[S4.7]: the Bank/Program pair is the one scheduled-controller
queue whose value a note-on reads back, and the read is a LOOK-AHEAD — at one
timestamp the note takes the last Program Change BEFORE THE NEXT NOTE EVENT
of that timestamp, not the one that preceded it in the byte stream.

=== The reach: probe 40 and probe 38

`[M: probe 40]`, 13 of 13 cases land within 1.9 dB of their control against
28-49 dB of separation between the three controls (`000:080` / `001:080` /
`008:080`, all program 80 so only the bank moves). The two cases that
separate this from plain stream order are case F (`b1 pc80, on60, b8 pc80`
→ `008:080`) and case G (`b8 pc80, on60, b1 pc80` → `001:080`):

[options="header"]
|===
| model | probe 40 score (of 13 cases)
| look-ahead (shipped) | 13/13
| plain stream order | 11/13
| order-independent "highest bank wins" | 8/13
|===

`[M: probe 38]` says the same for the program half, 7 of its 8 cases,
including its own case G — three layered locales at one tick on three keys,
`serum_opening`'s own shape, which collapses to the last group's patch on
all three notes and sits 19 dB from what stream order renders. Probe 38's
case F disagrees with probe 40's case G on byte-identical input — 1 outlier
in 21 observations across the two captures, unexplained and still `[O]`.

Probe 40's case K settles that this is the PROGRAM CHANGE queue and not the
bank byte: two Bank Selects with ONE Program Change and no queue tie at all
reads the last bank, in stream order. Cases L/M settle that a Bank Select
with no Program Change after it never reaches the note, so there is no live
bank read at voice-render time either.

=== Where the reach stops: probe 42

Probe 42 restages probe 40's cases across separate tracks the way a
sequencer lays out layered parts, and adds two shapes probe 40 structurally
cannot have: more than one note in a tick. Its own cases F/G still say the
read passes over a following group, but cases H (three groups, three keys)
and J (`serum_opening`'s Type B shape — two tracks playing both keys, a
third playing only the upper one, the last group the sine) both refute
reaching all the way to the end of the tick:

[options="header"]
|===
| probe 42 case | shape | stop-at-next-note (dB) | reach-to-end (dB) | plain stream order (dB)
| F | two groups | 3.67 | — | 28.65
| G | two groups | 3.29 | — | 30.69
| H | three groups, three keys | 3.15 | 12.05 | —
| J | `serum_opening` Type B (3 tracks) | 2.40 | 23.31 | —
|===

So the scope is the merged tick, not the track — but the reach stops at the
next note, it does not run to the end of the tick. Probe 40 cannot see this
difference, 13/13 either way, because it never puts two notes in one tick.
Probe means, ref-vs-build, for stream / reach-to-end / stop-at-next-note:
probe 42 is 8.98/5.68/2.70 dB, probe 38 is 10.20/9.01/8.89 dB (probe 38's
own case G improves 9.46 → 8.55).

=== This is a fit, not the recovered mechanism

Read directly from `artifacts/swmidi.sys`, the driver is unambiguously
reach-to-end:

* `[A:0x17fa2]` the KSMUSICFORMAT parser advances its 64-bit timestamp per
  BLOCK (`add [ebp-0x18],eax` / `adc [ebp-0x14],edx`, `0x18073`), never
  between messages inside one block, so a tick's messages tie exactly.
* `[A:0x13667]` note-on/off queue into `device+0x150` and `[A:0x132d2]`
  Program Change schedules into `device+0xc50+part*0x28`, same timestamp.
* `[A:0x16bae]` insert walks PAST every equal-timestamp node — FIFO.
* `[A:0x12bd6]` the drain, called once per audio block from the render
  callback `[A:0x130af]` with `now+nframes`, pops every due event in a loop
  (`0x1302a` → `0x16cac` → `jne 0x12c0b`).
* `[A:0x12dbc]` each note-on reads its locale at its OWN event timestamp via
  `0x16daa` → `[A:0x16c50]`, which walks the whole list and returns the LAST
  node with `ts <= req` (default: `object+0x18`).

Nothing there stops at a note. What the driver does NOT have is any
serialisation between MIDI parsing and rendering — they are separate KS pin
entry points (dispatch table at `0x1cbd4`) — so a note-on can drain before
the rest of its tick has been submitted. That is the likeliest source of what
probes 38/42 measured, and the only explanation offered so far for probe
38's case F answering differently to byte-identical input in two captures.
**Treat everything above as fitted to the captures on hand**; if the
references are re-recorded and probe 42's cases H/J stop reproducing, delete
this and go back to reach-to-end.

=== What shipped (`SMF_BANKPROG_LOOKAHEAD`, default 1)

The narrowest change that reproduces the captures: within one timestamp,
each NOTE event slides right over the run of Bank Select/Program Change
events immediately following it, preserving their order among themselves so
each Program Change still latches the bank byte that was live when it ran.
Nothing else moves — not SysEx, not tempo, not the other five
xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-2-1[S4.2.1] queues. Moving those too was measured and is
wrong twice over: it puts controllers ahead of a tick-0 GS Reset (wiping
it), and probe 37 already pinned same-tick CC7/CC121 behaviour that a
blanket hoist would disturb.

On `serum_opening` this restores the Type B saw layer the reach-to-end
reading deleted: its tick is `[t4 b1 pc80][n3][n15][t5 b1 pc81][n3'][n15'][t6
b8 pc80][n15'']`, and stopping at the next note leaves n3' on `001:081`,
which survives the same-key choke. Both gestures improve over reach-to-end
(Type A 10.8 → 9.3 dB, Type B 25.2 → 16.7 dB rms band error), and the two
field files densest in same-tick groups stop regressing (GENERAL_SERUM
-19.24 → -19.54, CrystalOscillator -24.66 → -24.96). Type B is still 16.7 dB
out, now uniformly too LOUD rather than too quiet — a level/patch question,
not an ordering one, and unresolved.

`SMF_BANKPROG_LOOKAHEAD` is an A/B guard for measurement only, same role as
xref:#item55[item 55]'s `SYNTH_SCHEDULE_LOCALE`; `0` restores plain stream
order. Not exposed by the Makefile.

'''

[#entry17]
== 2026-08-03 — Pitch-bend ramp DURATION: `RAMP_HORIZON_FRAMES = 320` (14.5 ms), fitted against probe 33's corpus mean, disagreeing ~40% with the per-rep edge measurement — entry17, SHIPPED

**Status: `[F:fitted]`.** Migrated from `src/engine/voice.c`'s largest
single comment block (161 lines, `RAMP_HORIZON_FRAMES`). `SPEC.adoc`
xref:SPEC.adoc#s6-6[S6.6]/xref:SPEC.adoc#s6-4-1[S6.4.1] `[A]`: the mixer
holds the phase step in a "ramp accumulator" re-derived from a
caller-supplied LINEAR step every `ramp_period` samples, held constant
between refreshes — not written directly (xref:#item19[item 19]'s prior
defect) and not a one-pole exponential (that shape is a separate,
already-fitted stand-in used only for GAIN, xref:#entry4[entry 4]; probes
28/32 already showed a one-pole is the wrong shape there too, but this fit
changes pitch only). What determines the caller's slope between calls is
genuinely `[O]` (S6.6), so the DURATION is measured, not derived.

=== Duration: flat across a 30x range of step sizes

`[M: probe 33]`, every condition the probe offers, read off
`artifacts/probe-results/33.flac` alone (the reference is the ground truth
here; the render is not consulted). 10-90% transition time, ensemble-averaged
over the 8 reps:

[options="header"]
|===
| step | ±200c | ±600c | ±2400c | ±4000c | 6000c (section C)
| ref 10-90% | 23-24 ms | 24 ms | 21-22 ms | 20-22 ms | 29-31 ms
|===

FLAT across a 30x range of step sizes — so the ramp is fixed-DURATION, not
the fixed-RATE slew this project shipped before (xref:#item19[item 19],
xref:#entry9[entry 9]). A rate law predicts ~2 ms for the 200c step and
~400 ms for the 6000c one; neither is observed. Section C's uptick to ~29 ms
is confounded (bigger step AND a mid-note re-aim rather than a fresh bend on
a settled note), so it does not carry the fit.

SHAPE is linear, same measurement: the 50% crossing lands at
`(t50-t10)/(t90-t10) = 0.43..0.54` in every condition, against 0.50 for a
linear ramp and 0.27 for a first-order exponential approach. 10-90% covers
80% of a linear ramp, so 22 ms of 10-90% is ~27.5 ms total; that figure and
the 320-frame value shipped below agree to well inside the ensemble spread.
Estimators: heterodyne (carrier at the key's centre frequency, low-pass,
unwrap+differentiate) for the ±200c/±600c steps, where FFT bin resolution is
far too coarse; per-cycle zero-crossing for the big steps, where a
heterodyne's target leaves the passband. FFT peak tracking, the obvious
third choice, is resolution-bound on both ends and under-reads a fast edge
by ~50%.

Two things this replaces, both now known to be wrong rather than merely
unconstrained: xref:#entry9[entry 9]'s single -24-semitone fit point
(16.51 ms, sd 3.90 ms) came from an FFT tracker and is the under-read above
— the same condition measures 21.3/21.7 ms here. And the old rate law scaled
off the voice's `phase_step` AT RAMP START, which made upward moves slow and
downward moves fast: probe 33's `C_square_17ms` shows the consequence
directly, the render's swing sat entirely on one side (+396..+2645c) instead
of straddling ±3000c. A duration law is symmetric by construction.

=== Period: `[M: probe 32 + probe 33, per-rep]`, 512 frames, 23.22 ms

Two independent readings land on it.

* **Duration.** Both probes' references glide a fixed 10-90% of
  16.4-16.8 ms for every step from 100 to 600 cents, 50% crossing at
  0.46-0.51 (linear), i.e. a full ramp of ~20.5-21 ms. Measured PER REP, not
  ensemble-averaged: the reference's bend-application time jitters ±18 ms rep
  to rep, and averaging edges that are each ~16.5 ms wide but mutually
  offset by up to 18 ms manufactures a ~23 ms composite that is the JITTER,
  not the ramp. An earlier pass of this project reported exactly that
  artifact (23.2/24.3 ms), and it is what the 27.5 ms figure above was fit
  to.
* **Grid.** Those same transition times concentrate on a period of 23.22 ms
  (circular concentration R=0.83) == 1024 samples at the 44.1 kHz the
  references were captured at == 512 samples at this driver's own 22050 Hz.
  The reference also renders a minority of reps (18 of 48 in probe 32) as
  near-instant steps at the estimator's floor, clustered at one end of that
  period — which is what a block grid does and a per-message countdown
  cannot do at all.

The bimodality itself is `[O]`: a plain "reach the target by the end of the
block" model predicts a continuum of durations, and the measured ones are
tightly bimodal at 4.2 and 16.5 ms with nothing between. Modelled here as the
uniform case; the instant minority is not reproduced.

=== Shape: fixed horizon per voice, not a shared grid

An earlier version of this file re-aimed every voice on a free-running
global clock — one refresh per period, and a mid-period message got whatever
was left of that period. That made two identical gestures traverse at
different speeds depending only on where the clock happened to be when the
message arrived — measured directly on `field/town.mid` (two near-identical
200-cent bend gestures, ~13 ms message spacing each, landing at different
phases of that clock: per-message slopes differed by up to 2x between the
two gestures with no musical cause) and on the corpus as a spectral ripple
at exactly the clock's period (`tests/radio.mid`, 8.0 cents peak-to-peak at
14.50ms — the period to the resolution of the estimator).

A held slope re-derived from the CURRENT value every period was also tried
(`SPEC.adoc`'s own "re-derived ... every `ramp_period` samples" read as
literally as possible); at any period other than the one chosen to size THIS
message's ramp, it either overshoots (short period, held past the point it
should have stopped) or degenerates into a never-settling exponential
(xref:#entry12[entry 12]'s geometric ramp, shown to reproduce here at
`horizon == period`). Every voice instead now gets its own fixed HORIZON,
sized once when a bend message moves its target and left alone until it
arrives — no shared clock, no periodic re-derivation, nothing to walk in and
out of phase with the music. `voice_ramp_tick` is a per-voice countdown that
retires an arrived ramp (`render.c` has no arrival test of its own); it no
longer re-aims anything (see also xref:#item52[item 52]).

Restarting a fixed duration per message never converges when messages arrive
faster than the duration either: it degenerates into a first-order lag whose
error GROWS with message density. Measured: `field/Kot_and_A64-GENERAL_SERUM.mid`
carries 36,763 bend events at a 6.7 ms median spacing, and `tests/radio.mid`
371 events at 8.0 ms, both far inside a ~27 ms duration — the reference's
kick pitch-drop is visibly and audibly quicker than a restarting ramp can
render, and radio's sweeps come out curved where the reference's are
straight. `tests/lazers.mid` is the same gesture as GENERAL_SERUM's, slowed
down (bends 41.7 ms apart, wider than the period), so a restarting ramp very
nearly completes and the file cannot distinguish the two models — do not fit
against it, fit against the dense files.

`ponytail:` one constant, no ceiling, no magnitude keying, no per-source
exemption — the measurement says duration does not depend on step size, so
nothing here should either.

=== Value shipped: the one honest wart in this fit

The corpus prefers **320 frames (14.5ms)**, not the 448-512 the per-rep edge
measurement above implies, and the shipped value is the corpus one. Swept
against the dedicated bend probes:

[options="header"]
|===
| | 192 | 256 | 320 | 384 | 448 | 512 | rate law (baseline)
| `33_ramp` | -27.86 | -31.45 | -34.04 | -32.75 | -32.29 | -30.39 | -30.04
| `05_bend` | -31.68 | -28.95 | -28.12 | -28.33 | -25.08 | -27.14 | -27.78
|===

320 is a clean optimum on `33_ramp` — the probe built for this question —
and beats the rate law it replaces by 4.0 dB there, the largest improvement
any variant of this ramp has produced. It disagrees with the edge
measurement (448-512 frames) by ~40%, which is not resolved. Reasons to
distrust the corpus side: the two items driving the corpus-mean regression,
`32_ramp_shape` and `field/HueArme-Weekend`, both have references whose
capture alignment is known bad — `32.flac` drifts 422 ms over 158 s
NON-linearly (43 ms residual to a straight line, 55 ms of spread within one
12-rep section), so no single lag aligns it and `score.py` uses exactly one.
Reasons to distrust the measurement side: none found, but it rests on one
analysis pass. **Re-derive both before treating 320 as recovered rather than
fitted.**

=== Still open: a possible rise/fall asymmetry

Not explained by any shape tried, this one included: `field/town.mid`'s two
bend gestures glide at measurably different rates depending on direction
(~2.2 vs ~4.24 cents/ms) at nearly the same message spacing. That may be a
real rise/fall asymmetry, or may be confounded with note age (the two
gestures sit at different offsets from note-on) — not separated. `[O]`. A
probe pairing a rise and a fall at equal note age would settle it; until
then this one constant is being asked to fit what may be two different
numbers.

'''

[#item52]
== 2026-08-03 — Pitch-bend ramp: companion mechanism findings (LSB precision, LFO/EG2 exclusion, instant re-aim) — item52, addendum to xref:#entry17[entry 17]

**Status: mechanism `[A]`/`[M]`, not itself a fitted value.** Three small
`src/engine/render.c`/`src/engine/voice.c` comment blocks migrated together
because all three explain design choices around xref:#entry17[entry 17]'s
`RAMP_HORIZON_FRAMES` fit rather than the fitted number itself.

=== 1/256-LSB ramp-step precision (`render.c`)

The mixer applies the held slope in 1/256 `phase_step` LSBs, not whole LSBs.
A whole-LSB slope cannot express this ramp at all below about a semitone: a
±2 semitone bend at key 72 moves ~420 Q12 LSBs, under 1 LSB per sample, and
truncating the slope to 1 silently turns the ramp into a rate limit —
measured, that made the period inert from 10 ms to 40 ms on probe 33's ±2
semitone conditions. The accumulator spends whole LSBs and keeps the
1/256ths remainder.

No arrival test lives in `render.c`'s per-sample accumulator itself:
`voice_ramp_tick` owns that, via a per-voice frame countdown rather than by
watching `phase_step` approach its target. Overshoot before the countdown
fires is bounded by one rounding step, since the slope was sized to land
exactly on arrival.

=== Only BEND is ramped (`voice.c`)

The period above is measured on bend steps and nothing else — probe 33 and
probe 32 send no LFO and no pitch envelope, so what the driver does with
those is `[O]`, and routing them through this ramp is a guess that measures
badly: **06_modwheel -3.2 dB, 26_other_gains -1.3 dB**, both files
containing no bend at all. Carried through instead by shifting `phase_step`
and its target by the SAME amount: the LFO/EG2 delta lands immediately while
whatever error the in-flight bend ramp is still carrying is preserved, so the
two do not fight over one accumulator.

=== Re-aim NOW, not the remainder of a shared clock (`voice.c`)

The bend is re-aimed the instant the target moves, over the FIXED horizon —
not the remainder of some shared clock's current period. Every bend gesture
of a given size therefore traverses at the same speed regardless of when it
happens to land, which a shared periodic clock cannot promise (see
xref:#entry17[entry 17]). No dead time either: a fresh voice's first bend is
re-aimed on the same call that moves its target, not left for a tick to
notice later.

'''

[#item53]
== 2026-08-03 — `dls.c`: internal-mechanism deviations from `SPEC.adoc`'s parse model (never observably different for gm.dls) — item53

**Status: `[I]`/`[O]`, no observable-output deviation for gm.dls.** Four
`src/engine/dls.c` comment blocks grouped: all describe places where this
parser's internal mechanism differs from what `SPEC.adoc` §2/§3 describes for
the original binary's mechanism, without changing the resolved
`Region`/`Wave` structures gm.dls itself produces.

=== Two-pass ptbl/wvpl resolution instead of a placeholder linked list

`SPEC.adoc` xref:SPEC.adoc#s2-8[S2.8] describes the original driver's
placeholder-linked-list-with-pointer-fixup mechanism for resolving `ptbl`/
`wvpl` references. This parser instead resolves them directly via a two-pass
offset array. The wave-level fmt/data/wsmp decode happens eagerly during
that pass, rather than at the deferred/unlocated point
xref:SPEC.adoc#s2-8-6[S2.8.6] flags as `[O]` in the original driver. Both
produce the same resolved `Region`→`Wave` structure for a well-formed file
such as gm.dls. Chunk-walk shape is unaffected and matches the driver: `next
= cur + 8 + size`, no RIFF odd-size padding except inside an INFO list where
size is rounded up to even (xref:SPEC.adoc#s2-2-1[S2.2.1]'s stated single
exception) — gm.dls's own chunks are all even-sized anyway, so this only
matters for defensive correctness on a hypothetical odd-sized foreign file.

=== `colh`/`vers`/`msyn`/`edit`: leniently size-validated

These four chunk types are parsed off with no state kept beyond size
validation, and that validation is deliberately lenient (a malformed size on
one of these four does not fail the load). Not exercised by gm.dls, which
carries well-formed instances of all four.

=== DLS-1 wsmp inheritance fallback, never exercised by gm.dls, `[O]` in general

A region with no wsmp chunk of its own falls back to its wave's
wsmp-derived unity note / fine tune — the standard DLS-1 region-overrides-wave
convention. `SPEC.adoc` xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-3-2[S3.3.2] flags this exact
fallback path as never exercised by gm.dls (every one of its **1498
regions** carries its own wsmp) and, for the driver in general, `[O]` — this
is a DLS-1-spec inference, not disassembly-confirmed. No change for gm.dls;
present for correctness on any other DLS bank.

=== The key-range walk is part of `FindInstrument` itself

`[A:0x147b7]`: the key-range walk is part of `FindInstrument`, not a step
after it — an instrument whose regions do not cover the requested note is
rejected exactly like a locale mismatch, so the caller's next fallback tier
gets a turn. This is what makes a drum kit with a hole in its key map
(gm.dls's SFX kit, program 56, covers only keys 39-84) fall through to the
Standard kit for a missing key instead of dropping the note, matching
xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-1-3[S3.1.3]'s three-tier fallback.

'''

[#item54]
== 2026-08-03 — `voice.c`: small migrated notes (reserve-tier tagging, cents-clamp rationale, `DECAY_RATE_MULT` removal, `exp_coef_scaled` re-verification, mixer priming) — item54

**Status: mixed `[I]`/`[O]`/measurement addenda, grouped for size.** Five
small `src/engine/voice.c` comment blocks with no natural larger home.

=== Reserve-tier starting index is cosmetic

`SPEC.adoc` xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-2[S5.2]/S5.5's 48/6 split: which physical
array index starts primary vs. reserve is cosmetic/`[O]` — nothing
downstream distinguishes a voice's tier once it becomes active, and
`voice_topup_reserve`'s own Branch A retags nodes over time anyway (see
xref:#item7[item 7]).

=== `±CENTS_CLAMP` left at ±4800 after RPN1/RPN2 were added to the cents sum

The clamp itself (`±4800`, byte-confirmed) is already in `SPEC.adoc`
xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-3-3[S3.3.3]. This addendum is the rejected-alternative
reasoning: the clamp was left as-is even after adding RPN1/RPN2 into the
cents sum, because the reference file's actual RPN2 usage (±1200/±2400
cents) plus ordinary bend/base_cents stays far under 4800, and raising the
clamp would deviate from a confirmed hardware behaviour, not fix a bug. Also
applied to EG2's pitch contribution in `voice_step_eg2` — same hardware
bound, same table domain.

=== `DECAY_RATE_MULT` was removed, not merely left unused (addendum to xref:#entry15[entry 15])

SUPERSEDED 2026-07-26 by `SPEC.adoc` xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-1-2-1[S5.1.2.1]: the
macro was a rate multiplier on the OLD asymptotic approach-to-target decay
shape, entangled with that shape's own "100 dB over seconds" calibration.
The decay segment is now a geometric ramp whose coefficient is derived
directly from `env_sustain_level` and the already-rescaled
decay-to-sustain duration — there is no free rate parameter left to scale,
both endpoints are pinned by the target/duration formulas. The macro was
removed rather than left dead; xref:#entry15[entry 15]'s 0.965 measurement
stays on record as history.

=== `exp_coef_scaled` `mult=1.0` re-verified 2026-07-25 under the corrected aligner (addendum to xref:#entry1[entry 1]/xref:#entry15[entry 15])

`mult` is a rate multiplier, 1.0 = the shipped calibration.
xref:#entry1[Entry 1] fit 2.85 for the decay segment against probe 04 and did
not ship it. Re-tested 2026-07-25 under the corrected harness aligner (see
"Harness re-baseline, 2026-07-25" above; decay, release, and both segments at
2.85) and the shipped 1.0 still wins on the corpus mean, probe 04, GENERAL
SERUM and envelope r — so that rejection was not an artifact of the old
broken alignment. Kept overridable so the sweep is one `-D` away rather than
an edit.

=== `amp_l`/`amp_r` primed from the envelope's starting level

Matches the driver's own `nsamples==0` mixer priming: `[A:0x18fba]` called
with `nsamples==0` writes the gains straight into the ramp state without
rendering, `[A:0x18fd9]`. Leaving them at zero instead makes every
instant-attack patch (`attack_s <= 0` sets `env_level = 1.0`) fade in across
a whole segment — measured as a 23 ms ramp-up on every note of probe 14, 5x
too quiet at 4 ms in.

'''

[#item55]
== 2026-08-03 — `synth.c`/`synth.h`: small migrated notes (`SYNTH_SCHEDULE_LOCALE` A/B confound, bend-cents `sar` vs `/` and int64 bound, running-status) — item55

**Status: measurement-harness provenance / `[I]` rationale, not behavioral
findings.** Three small `src/engine/synth.c`/`synth.h` comment blocks grouped
for size.

=== `SYNTH_SCHEDULE_LOCALE` A/B guard, confounded by a concurrent change (addendum to xref:#item14[item 14])

`SYNTH_SCHEDULE_LOCALE`, default 1 (shipped), selects xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-2-1[S4.2.1]'s
scheduled locale, latched only at Program Change/reset (xref:#item14[item 14]).
`0` restores the prior behavior this item replaces (recompute live from
`bank_msb`/`bank_lsb`/`program` on every call). This macro exists so the two
behaviors can be A/B'd from the exact same tree state: a concurrent,
unrelated change landed in `voice.c`/`render.c` during the same work, so this
item's own effect could not be cleanly isolated from that concurrent work by
diffing two builds — flipping this single macro and rebuilding twice in a
row can be. Not a permanent feature flag; not exposed by the Makefile.

=== Pitch-bend-cents: `sar` vs `/` and the int64-vs-int32 magnitude bound

`(raw * rangeCents) >> 13`, FLOOR, matches `SPEC.adoc`
xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-3-2[S3.3.2](c)'s stated "arithmetic right shift —
truncation toward negative infinity, not toward zero" — confirmed, not a
deviation (see xref:#s3-5-pitch-cents-correction[s3-5-pitch-cents-correction]).
S4.4's "C-style truncation toward zero" is a different formula entirely:
RPN1 Channel Fine Tuning's `((combined14bit - 8192) * 100) / 8192`, not this
one (see also xref:#item56[item 56]'s citation-mislabel note on this same
distinction, found independently in `synth.c`'s own comment above this
function).

Two rejected-alternative facts recorded here: `sar` (this shift) floors,
`/` rounds toward zero — they differ by one cent on every downward bend that
is not an exact multiple of 8192, which is most of them. And int64 here vs.
the driver's 32-bit multiply is not itself a difference: `|raw| <= 8192` and
`range <= 4800` peaks at 39.3M, well inside int32 either way.

=== Running-status expansion is the caller's job

`synth_midi()` dispatches one short MIDI message with an explicit status
byte; running-status expansion, if any, is the caller's own responsibility.
`status == 0xFF` is treated as System Reset.

'''

[#item56]
== 2026-08-03 — `unit.c`: small migrated notes (golden-data provenance, Table D float32-vs-double, a citation mislabel in `synth.c`, one gm.dls data point) — item56

**Status: measurement-harness provenance / a documentation-accuracy note,
not behavioral findings.** Four small `src/unit.c` comment blocks grouped
for size.

=== Golden test-vector provenance

The golden data arrays are mechanically extracted from
`SCRATCH/golden_tables.h` by `SCRATCH/gen_golden.py`, and verified
byte-for-byte round-trip against the source by `SCRATCH/verify_golden.py`.
Neither script nor `SCRATCH/` is present in this repository — historical
provenance narration of a check that was run when the golden arrays were
generated, not a pointer to a file that exists now. Do not hand-edit the
golden arrays.

=== Table D matches SPEC despite a float32-vs-double `2*pi` constant

`tables_build()` populates all 256 Table D (sine LFO) entries identically to
SPEC's golden dump (mism=0), including at i=64/i=192. `tables.c` uses the
full double-precision `2*pi` literal (`6.28318530717958647692`) rather than
the binary's stored float32-rounded `2*pi` (`0x11d18` =
`6.2831854820251465`), but that constant mismatch never survives `rt_sin`'s
own Taylor-series undershoot plus the final trunc-to-int16 at any of the 256
sampled phases — verified by direct execution over all 256 phases, not
merely at two spot-checked indices.

=== `synth.c` mis-cites S4.4 instead of S3.3.2(c) for the bend-cents floor

`src/engine/synth.c`'s own comment above `synth_pitch_bend_cents` attributes
the floor (arithmetic-right-shift) behaviour to `SPEC.adoc` S4.4. S4.4 is
actually about the *RPN1* Channel Fine Tuning formula (truncation toward
zero), a different formula (see xref:#item55[item 55]). The correct citation
is xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-3-2[S3.3.2](c), whose text reads, verbatim: "with an
arithmetic right shift — truncation toward negative infinity, not toward
zero." That IS floor, and it is exactly what `synth.c`'s `>>13` computes. No
live disagreement: this is a citation correction in `synth.c`'s own comment,
not a behavioral fix, and does not change any `unit.c` assertion.

=== gm.dls instrument `0x1007a` pan_cb data point

Instrument locale `0x1007a` (bank MSB 4, program 122): its shared
instrument-level `lart` carries exactly one `(0,0,0x0002)` connection,
`lScale=-8`, and one `(0,0,0x0004)` connection, `lScale=-524288` (raw values
read independently via `SCRATCH/riff_walk.py`, also not present in this
repository). Per xref:SPEC.adoc#s2-4-2[S2.4.2]/xref:SPEC.adoc#s2-4-3[S2.4.3]:
dest `0x0002` → `(lScale<<4)/125` = `(-8<<4)/125` = `-128/125` = `-1`
(truncated toward zero); dest `0x0004` → `(lScale>>12)/125` =
`(-524288>>12)/125` = `-128/125` = `-1`. Both write the same `pan_cb` field,
and both formulas happen to agree at `-1` for this instrument's real data, so
file order between the two connections doesn't matter for this data point.

'''

value. Recorded as a third data point for whichever future pass revisits
`GAIN_SEGMENT_FRAMES`.

'''

[#item50]
== 2026-08-02 — The constant-power pan law reproduces all nine S3.6 anchors to 0.05 dB once the sum clamp is included; `o4` is resolved, the anchor tests were fixtured to avoid the clamp, and the gain path now runs on the two tables the engine already built — item50, SHIPPED

**Status: SHIPPED.** Resolves `SPEC.adoc`'s `o4`, retires
xref:#item45[item 45]'s nine-anchor interpolation, lands
xref:#item47[item 47]'s `+1200`, and turns `unit.c` test 173 green.
Supersedes xref:#item49[item 49]'s "why it does not ship" section, which
rested on an arithmetic error corrected below.

=== The floor discrepancy was an arithmetic error

item 49 reported the law's hard-pan floor as `PAN[0] − PAN[64]` = −22.03 dB
against a measured −20.20 dB and left a 1.8 dB gap open as `o4`. That
subtraction is wrong. The near channel is not sitting at `PAN[64]` — it is
pinned at `TABLE[0]` by the `0x18dea` sum clamp. The observable floor is
`sum + PAN[0]` relative to that pin, where `sum` is the pre-pan attenuation
accumulated for the sounding note.

=== Probe 25 re-measured, and the law fits all nine anchors

`artifacts/probe-results/25.flac` re-measured directly (nine 1.0 s notes,
onsets detected at 0.19 s + k, RMS over onset+0.10 s to onset+0.35 s). It
reproduces S3.6's published table to within **0.02 dB at every anchor**, so
the measurement was never in doubt. Two facts it settles:

* The file is **digitally silent between notes** (−240 dBFS, i.e. exactly
  zero), so there is no capture noise floor and no channel bleed that could
  have shallowed the measured floor.
* The near channel sits at **−10.98 dBFS across CC10 0–80**, unchanged from
  centre to hard pan. That flat is the clamp pin, observed directly.

Fitting the law `gain = TABLE[clamp((sum + PAN[j])/10, −1000, 0)]` to SPEC's
own table with `sum` as the **single** free parameter:

[options="header"]
|===
| CC10 | predicted L | SPEC L | predicted R | SPEC R
| 0 | 0.00 | 0.00 | −20.24 | −20.20
| 16 | 0.00 | 0.00 | −4.23 | −4.20
| 32 | 0.00 | 0.00 | −1.22 | −1.20
| 48 / 64 / 80 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.00
| 96 | −1.36 | −1.41 | 0.00 | 0.00
| 112 | −4.51 | −4.52 | 0.00 | 0.00
| 127 | −20.24 | −20.21 | 0.00 | 0.00
|===

Eighteen measured values, one parameter, **worst anchor error 0.05 dB** at
`sum` = +476 hundredths of a dB above the clamp point. The measured table is
therefore not a law in its own right — it is this law sampled at one
operating point.

=== Why the anchor tests could not have passed

`src/unit.c`'s S3.6 anchor block set `atten_const_hdb = 0` and carried a
diagnostic stating the gains "must sit strictly below the ceiling or this
measures the clamp, not the pan law". The fixture deliberately operated where
the clamp cannot engage, on the premise that the clamp was an artefact to be
excluded. That premise is inverted: at probe 25's operating point the clamp
*is* the observable law, and with it excluded the near channel is free to
rise, so the driver's own law cannot reproduce the driver's own anchors.

The fixture now drives the same conditions probe 25 does — Sine patch (bank 8
program 80), CC7 and CC11 at their GM defaults, velocity 100, the region's
real attenuation — and asserts the anchors against that.

=== What shipped

The engine already built both tables and used neither in the gain path:

* `g_table_lin[128]` (`tables.c:49-53`) is `PAN[j] = trunc(1000·log10(j/127))`
  with `PAN[0] = −2500` — byte-identical to the driver's pan table at
  `0x1bfd0`, and already asserted byte-exact by the §T.3 test.
* `g_table_dbamp[1001]` (`tables.c:57-59`) is
  `trunc(4095·sqrt(10^(i/100)))` — the `0x18dea` conversion table, already
  asserted byte-exact by the §T.1 test.

`voice_update_gain` now sums pan into the attenuation before conversion and
reads both, and `voice_note_on` adds the `+1200`:

[source,c]
----
atten = master + chanVol + expr + v->atten_const_hdb;   // atten_const_hdb includes +1200
i     = clamp((atten + PAN[127 - pan]) / 10, -1000, 0);
gainL = TABLE[i] / 8192.0;                              // denominator per item49
----

Deleted with it: the nine-anchor table and its interpolator (item 45), the
`GAIN_CEILING` output clamp (the table's own ceiling replaces it), the
`GAIN_TRIM_DB` hook, and the `ATTEN_PLUS_1200` gate. No new table, no fitted
constant, and `[F]` count falls by one.

xref:#item45[Item 45] is superseded rather than merely replaced:
`g_table_lin` was the driver's pan table all along, so the near-channel rise
item 45 removed as a defect was real behaviour, and what holds the near
channel at unity is the clamp, not the `- g_table_lin[63]` pivot it deleted.

=== Corpus

[options="header"]
|===
| item | before | after | Δresidual
| MEAN | 0.928 / −31.53 dB | **0.932 / −32.48 dB** | **−0.95 dB (better)**
| `45_release_from_level` | −25.71 dB | −49.92 dB | −24.21 dB (better)
| `27_gain_curves` | −27.51 dB | −49.59 dB | −22.08 dB (better)
| `26_other_gains` | −29.53 dB | −36.08 dB | −6.55 dB (better)
| `50_gain_ceiling` | −27.13 dB | −32.79 dB | −5.66 dB (better)
| `07_pan_volume` | −42.15 dB | −45.68 dB | −3.53 dB (better)
| `25_pan_law` | −51.71 dB | −51.72 dB | ±0.01 dB
| `41_sustain_decay_curve` | −51.01 dB | −37.11 dB | **+13.90 dB (worse)**
|===

15 of 68 items improve by more than 1 dB, 5 regress. `25_pan_law` is
unchanged: the law reproduces probe 25 exactly as well as the nine-anchor
interpolation fitted to it did, without being fitted to anything.

**The one regression is known and is not this item's.** `41_sustain_decay_curve`
is xref:#item49[item 49]'s two-population split: `GAIN_CEILING` engages on
its three zero-region-attenuation voices where the reference does not clamp,
and no uniform attenuation closes that against the program-80-at-127 items
that need the clamp exactly where it is. It lands as a known regression
because holding the whole correction hostage to it would keep 15 items wrong
to keep 1 item right. The discriminating terms are region/wsmp attenuation
and the `g_table_vel` lookup at 100 versus 127.

'''

[#item49]
== 2026-08-02 — S3.6's pan law is ONE table read forward and reversed, not two; the measured "unity at centre" is the attenuation-sum clamp, not the pan law; and the engine is a confirmed 2.94 dB quiet — item49, SPEC corrected

**Status: `SPEC.adoc` §3.6 corrected.** The engine change this item deferred
landed in xref:#item50[item 50]; its "why it does not ship" section below is
**superseded** — the 1.8 dB floor it reported was an arithmetic error, and
`o4` is resolved, not open.

=== Correction to xref:#item48[item 48]: the corpus residual cannot see level

`artifacts/score.py`'s `residual_db` RMS-normalizes both signals before
taking spectra (`b = avg_spectrum(slop · tgt/sr)` with `sr = RMS(slop)`) and
returns `‖a−b‖/‖a‖`, which cancels any scale common to both. Envelope `r` is
scale-free by construction. **A uniform gain on the render cannot move
either number.** item 48's trim sweep therefore did not locate a level; it
located the point at which the `GAIN_CEILING` clamp begins to engage. Its
conclusion — that the engine runs ~3 dB quiet — is correct, but the evidence
cited for it does not support it. The evidence that does is in §4 below.

The rest of item 48 stands: the pairing really is a pure level shift, and
cancelling its net really does reproduce the default build, which is exactly
what gain-invariance predicts.

=== The pan law reads ONE table, forward and reversed

SPEC §3.6 previously read `0x19c12`–`0x19c2a` as a *two-table asymmetric*
law, `gainA` from the linear/√ table and `gainB` from `table_1c9d0` (the
squared-law table). That is wrong. The two base addresses differ by exactly
`0x1c1cc − 0x1bfd0 = 0x1FC = 127·4`, i.e. they are the two ends of a single
128-entry table:

[source,c]
----
sum  += 1200;                        // 0x19bcd  lea 0x4b0(%ecx,%eax,1)
pan   = clamp(pan, 0, 127);          // 0x19c04-0x19c0f
sumA  = sum + PAN[127 - pan];        // 0x19c15-0x19c1e  base 0x1c1cc, descending
sumB  = sum + PAN[pan];              // 0x19c24-0x19c2a  base 0x1bfd0, ascending
----

Both results are hundredths of a dB and both go through the same conversion
at `0x18dea`. The squared-law table is not involved. The builder at
`0x16a3e`:

[source,c]
----
for (j = 1; j <= 127; j++)               // 0x16a55/57/63, dst 0x1bfd4 = &PAN[1]
    PAN[j] = (int)(log10f(j / 127.0f)    // 0x16a67 fmuls 1/127; 0x16a6d-71 fldlg2/fyl2x
                   * 1000.0f);           // 0x16a73 fmuls 1000.0; 0x16a79 ftol
PAN[0] = -2500;                          // 0x16a8a  movl $0xfffff63c,0x1bfd0
----

so `amp = 10^(PAN/2000) = sqrt(j/127)` — a **constant-power** law. This
retires the old reading's two absurdities: centre is `PAN[63]`/`PAN[64]` =
−3.04/−2.97 dB, not −3.0/−11.9 dB, and the extremes are a finite −25 dB
floor, not silence.

=== "Unity at centre" is the attenuation-sum clamp, not the pan law

`0x18dea` indexes its table as `TABLE[clamp(atten_hdb/10, −1000, 0)]`. The
upper clamp at 0 means any accumulated sum at or above the table's ceiling
pins to `TABLE[0]`. Under probe 25's conditions both channels sit at that
pin near centre, so the *measured* curve shows unity on both sides and a
wide flat plateau from CC10≈48 to ≈80 — while the underlying law is
constant-power throughout. The plateau is the clamp.

This is checkable and was checked: building the full driver law (constant-power
pan table, `+1200`, `4095/8192` ceiling, sum clamp) turns `unit.c`'s test
175, "CC10=64 is unity on BOTH channels simultaneously", from RED to GREEN,
along with 176 and 177. No fitted term is involved.

=== The engine runs 2.94 dB quiet — measured on absolute levels

Three constants compose to a net level: `+1200` (S3.5) less the conversion
table's own ceiling `4095/8192` (−6.02 dB) less the pan table's centre value
`PAN[63]` (−3.04 dB) = **+2.94 dB**. Measured directly on
`41_sustain_decay_curve`, whole-file, against `artifacts/probe-results/41.flac`:

[options="header"]
|===
| build | peak | RMS | vs reference (peak / RMS)
| reference | −6.83 dBFS | −25.38 dBFS | —
| shipped default | −9.87 dBFS | −28.32 dBFS | **−3.04 / −2.94 dB**
| default +2.94 dB | −7.59 dBFS | −25.71 dBFS | −0.76 / −0.33 dB
|===

The shipped engine is 2.94 dB (RMS) below the reference against a
prediction of 2.94 dB, arrived at from the disassembly with nothing fitted.
This is an absolute-level measurement and is independent of the
gain-invariant corpus metric.

=== The full driver law, measured

Scratch build, `voice_update_gain` replaced by the law above, full 68-item
corpus:

[options="header"]
|===
| item | default | driver law | Δ
| MEAN | 0.928 / −31.53 dB | **0.932 / −32.48 dB** | −0.95 dB (better)
| `45_release_from_level` | −25.71 dB | −49.92 dB | −24.21 dB (better)
| `27_gain_curves` | −27.51 dB | −49.59 dB | −22.08 dB (better)
| `50_gain_ceiling` | −27.13 dB | −32.79 dB | −5.66 dB (better)
| `07_pan_volume` | −42.15 dB | −45.68 dB | −3.53 dB (better)
| `25_pan_law` | −51.71 dB | −51.72 dB | ±0.01 dB
| `41_sustain_decay_curve` | −51.01 dB | −37.11 dB | +13.90 dB (worse)
|===

`25_pan_law` is unchanged, i.e. the constant-power table reproduces probe
25 exactly as well as the shipped nine-anchor interpolation does, without
being fitted to it.

=== Why it did not ship at the time — SUPERSEDED by xref:#item50[item 50]

Two failures were reported. The first was an arithmetic error and the second
is unchanged; both are recorded as written for provenance.

. **[WITHDRAWN] The hard-pan floor is 1.8 dB too deep.** `PAN[0] = −2500` against
  `PAN[64] = −297` gives −22.03 dB where S3.6 measures **−20.20 dB** and
  calls it "a real floor, not an unmeasured tail". `unit.c` tests 178, 179
  and 180 fail on this; 180 reports −21.98 against −20.20. The store was
  re-read byte-for-byte during this pass (`movl $0xfffff63c,0x1bfd0`,
  `0xfffff63c` = −2500) and is not in doubt, so the discrepancy is between
  a confirmed instruction and a confirmed measurement. **Unresolved.**
  Note that −22.03 dB is precisely what the pre-xref:#item45[item 45]
  `g_table_lin` law produced, which suggests `g_table_lin` was this same
  table all along and item 45 replaced a correct shape with a measured one.
. **`41_sustain_decay_curve` regresses 13.90 dB**, for the reason in §
  below.

Under the SPEC-prevails rule an engine change may not land while it fails
`unit.c` assertions that SPEC still endorses. The SPEC correction above is
`[A]` and lands now; the law itself waits on the floor.

=== The clamp splits the corpus into two populations that no single setting fits

Established by isolating `41_sustain_decay_curve`. Its damage is entirely
`GAIN_CEILING` engaging: removing the clamp restores the default residual
exactly (−51.06 vs −51.01), clamping begins at the predicted +2.279 dB
threshold, and the residual is flat to 0.04 dB below it. Exactly 3 of its 8
notes clamp, and those three are the only ones with region attenuation of
exactly 0. Sweeping the ceiling at fixed level:

[options="header"]
|===
| clamp ceiling | `27_gain_curves` | `41_sustain_decay` | `45_release_from_level`
| +0.00 dB (0.4999) | −49.61 dB | −37.67 dB | −48.45 dB
| +0.75 dB (0.5451) | −40.69 dB | −51.06 dB | −32.62 dB
| +2.00 dB (0.6294) | −31.58 dB | −51.06 dB | −25.73 dB
|===

`41` is fully recovered by +0.75 dB of ceiling; `27` and `45` degrade
monotonically from the same point. The two populations are structurally
distinct: the ones wanting the ceiling at 0.4999 are program 80 (Sine,
region attenuation −314 hdB) driven at 127, and the ones wanting no clamp
are the zero-region-attenuation voices at velocity 100. They disagree by
**0.7–1.1 dB**, so no uniform attenuation anywhere in the chain closes both.
The discriminating terms are region/wsmp attenuation and the `g_table_vel`
lookup at 100 versus 127 — which is where xref:#item47[item 47]'s
term-by-term comparison against the driver's `atten_sum` should start.

=== Also corrected

* The raw gain register's denominator is **8192**. The mixer does
  `acc = raw << 8` (`0x19e95`), `gain_Q = acc >> 5` (`0x19e9f`), then
  `pmulhw` (`0x19f56`), a net `>>13`; the same instruction triple appears in
  all four mixer functions (`0x1a05d`, `0x1a22f`, `0x1a3eb`). So
  `gain_linear = raw/8192` and `TABLE[0] = 4095` → 0.49988, which lands on
  `GAIN_CEILING`'s `32767/65536` to within 0.002 dB. An earlier suspicion
  that the ceiling might be a ÷8 (`4095/32767`, −18.06 dB) is **wrong** — it
  compares the raw register against sample full scale, skipping the `<<3`.
* The conversion table at `0x1bfc0` is **built at init by `0x16680`**, not
  static; its on-disk bytes are zero. Its index is signed, with index 0 at
  `0x1bfc0` and the body running down to `0x1b020`.
* `0x19fae` (`psrld $3`) discards the 8 fractional bits of the 24.8 ramp
  accumulator at each render-call boundary, so §6.4.5's "net `<<3>>3` =
  identity" holds only for calls in which no ramp step occurred. Sub-LSB
  gain state does not survive a buffer boundary. Too small to matter for
  any measurement here.

'''

xref:#item47[Item 47] asked for a term-by-term comparison of this engine's

right search, but it is now a search for a **single missing attenuation of
roughly 300 hundredths of a dB**, not for a diffuse composition error: any
correct answer has to leave the shape alone, because the shape is already
right.

. The re-anchor used `GAIN_CEILING = 32767/65536` (a ÷2), but the decoded

=== What a future pass should check

The `+1200` and the `0x18dea`/`4095` table are both `[A]`, not guesses; the
gap is in how this engine's own `atten_hdb` gets *composed* before it
reaches that shape, not in the shape itself. A future pass should compare,
term by term, this engine's `atten_hdb = g_master_vol_hdb + chan_vol + expr
+ v->atten_const_hdb` (`voice_update_gain`) against the driver's own
`atten_sum` accumulation at the disassembly level — master volume, channel
volume, expression (`g_table_vel`), and region/velocity attenuation each
have their own conversion path, and item47's regression shape (two items
off by >25 dB, most by 5–15 dB, a few improving) reads like a second,
uncorrected composition error compounding with the `+1200`, not like the
`+1200` being wrong on its own. `xref:#item40[Item 40]`'s already-noted
"~1.6–1.7 dB hotter" flat-top residual is the most likely place to start.

=== The `+1200` was never in doubt — what it feeds into was

xref:#item35[Item 35] identified the constant and deferred it: *"it
interacts with the still-`[O]` mapping from hundredths-of-a-dB to the raw
Q16 gain register (§6.4.5)... so it is plainly compensated somewhere
downstream"* — but no compensation was ever found in `src/`, because none
exists there (`GAIN_TRIM_DB` is confirmed `0.0` in the shipped source, and
no other code path touches `atten_hdb` before the exponential). The
"downstream" that compensates for it is inside the driver's own conversion
table, not this project's engine.

=== The hdB→raw-gain conversion, decoded — resolves `[#o16]`

`0x18dea` is the function every `atten_hdb` value (region attenuation,
velocity term, channel volume/expression, master volume, pan — all summed
in the hundredths-of-a-dB domain per xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-5[§3.5]) is run
through before it becomes the persistent gain state at `voice+0x2c`/`+0x30`:

[source,c]
----
i = atten_hdb / 10;              // 0x18df6: idiv 10
if (i < -1000) i = -1000;        // 0x18dfd/0x18dff
if (i >= 0)     i = 0;           // 0x18e05/0x18e07
raw_gain = TABLE[i];             // 0x18e0b: mov eax,[eax*4+0x1bfc0]
----

`TABLE` is **not a new table** — it is xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-3-1[§3.3.1]'s
already-`[A]` "dB→linear amplitude" table at `0x1bfc0` (`n = −1000..0`,
1001 entries, `trunc(4095·sqrt(10^(n/100)))`), read here with the plain
attenuation-sum index that section left unconnected to any consumer. The
domain clamp above (`i` held to `[−1000, 0]`) is exactly what makes
`atten_hdb > 0` harmless: it never indexes past the table's own built-in
ceiling, `TABLE[0] = 4095`.

Combined with xref:SPEC.adoc#s6-4-5[§6.4.5]'s already-confirmed `<<3`
rest-to-active scaling and the Q16 multiply (`raw_gain<<3` divided by
`65536`), `TABLE[0] = 4095` is the field's real, driver-defined ceiling:
`4095·8 / 65536 = 32760/65536 ≈ 0.499969` of computed unity — matching
xref:SPEC.adoc#s6-4-5-1[§6.4.5.1]'s independently-derived hardware bound
(`32767/65536 ≈ 0.499985`, from the `packssdw` signed-16 lane alone) to
within **7 parts in 65536, ~0.002 dB**. `4095 = floor(32767/8)`: the
table's own scale constant (float32 `4095.0`
at `0x11cd8`, byte-decoded `00 f0 7f 45`) is the largest integer whose
`<<3` never trips the `packssdw` saturation the mixer already enforces
downstream — not a coincidence, a second, independent confirmation of the
same bound from the table-builder side rather than the mixer side.

**`[#o16]` is answered: the raw gain field's absolute fixed-point unit is
not an independent Q-format choice at all — it is this table's own output,
whose ceiling is forced to coincide with the `packssdw` bound by
construction.** Retired from the open-questions register; see
xref:SPEC.adoc#s6-4-5[§6.4.5] for the resolved passage.

=== Why the `+1200` does not cancel

`TABLE[i=0] = 4095` with no bias term in the table's own formula — the
table's zero point is `atten_hdb = 0`, not `atten_hdb = 1200`. So:

----
raw_gain = TABLE( clamp( (atten_sum_before_1200 + 1200) / 10, -1000, 0 ) )
----

For any note whose un-shifted sum is within 12 dB of "fully open"
(`atten_sum_before_1200 > -1200`), the `+1200` pushes the index past the
table's domain and the result clamps to `4095` — this is the mechanism, not
a flat +12 dB everywhere. Only deep in the attenuation range
(`atten_sum_before_1200 < -1200`, below the table's own domain floor) does
it act as an uncancelled, unclamped shift.

=== The paired engine edit — kept behind `ATTEN_PLUS_1200`, default off

[source,c]
----
// default (ATTEN_PLUS_1200 == 0, voice.c ~1304, ~919)
v->atten_const_hdb = scaled + (int32_t)r->attenuation_hdb;
double gain_linear = rt_pow(10.0, ((double)atten_hdb/100.0 + GAIN_TRIM_DB)/20.0);  // GAIN_TRIM_DB == 0.0, inert

// -DATTEN_PLUS_1200=1 (rejected pairing, this item)
v->atten_const_hdb = scaled + (int32_t)r->attenuation_hdb + 1200;   // SPEC S3.5 [A:0x19bcd]
double gain_linear = GAIN_CEILING * rt_pow(10.0, (double)atten_hdb / 2000.0);
----

Both lines are gated on the same `ATTEN_PLUS_1200` macro so the pair can
only be on or off together, never mixed. `GAIN_TRIM_DB` stays as the
default path's hook — both prior attempts to fit it nonzero
(xref:#item39[Item 39], `-1.7`; xref:#item40[Item 40], `+1.37`) were
already superseded before this item (neither touched the domain-clamp
*shape* this item's `-DATTEN_PLUS_1200=1` path implements, and
`score.py`'s residual is level-normalized enough that a flat trim barely
moved it), so it is left at its confirmed `0.0` rather than reintroduced as
a knob. The `GAIN_CEILING` constant used on the `ATTEN_PLUS_1200=1` side
(`32767.0/65536.0`) is kept rather than replaced with the table's exact
`4095.0/8192.0` — the two agree to ~0.002 dB (see the decode above) and
`GAIN_CEILING` is also the number xref:SPEC.adoc#s6-4-5-1[§6.4.5.1] derives
independently from the mixer's own `packssdw` bound, so keeping it does not
trade one unverified constant for another, if a future pass gets this
pairing working.

`unit.c` test 174 ("region's own (wsmp) attenuation is summed once")
tracks the DEFAULT build, i.e. `atten_const_hdb - scaled ==
region.attenuation_hdb` (the `+1200` is not part of it). Test 173
("atten_const_hdb == scaled + region.attenuation_hdb + 1200") asserts
`SPEC.adoc`'s own summed pseudocode directly and stays RED against the
default build — an intentional, standing failure, not weakened or removed,
per this project's rule that `SPEC.adoc` wins over `src/` and a `unit.c`
assertion is never loosened to match what the engine happens to do.

[#item46]
== 2026-08-01 — Table C's builder was missing `log10` (a parabola, not a dB curve); its sole reader is EvalEnvelope's attack branch, and a quantized-attack A/B did not improve the corpus — item46, SHIPPED (table fix); quantized-attack variant MEASURED, NOT SHIPPED (default OFF)

**Status: SHIPPED** for the `tables.c` fix (tests 8/10 RED->GREEN). **MEASURED,
NOT SHIPPED** for the optional `ENV_ATTACK_TABLE_C` quantized-attack variant
-- default stays OFF, see the A/B section below.

**Path:** `artifacts/INVESTIGATE.adoc` `[#i1]`; SPEC.adoc
xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-4-2[S3.4.2], xref:SPEC.adoc#t4[T.4] (both already correct,
no SPEC.adoc change this pass); `src/engine/tables.c` (Table C builder),
`src/engine/voice.c` (`ENV_ATTACK_TABLE_C` guard).

**Tests:** 8 (byte-exact 201-entry Table C dump vs. SPEC.adoc's golden
array), 10 (SPEC's own `t[200] -> 999` check). Both RED before, GREEN after.

=== The bug

`tables.c`'s Table C loop had no `log10` call at all -- a parabola
(`1000 + x^2*10000/96`), not SPEC's dB curve (`1000 + log10(x^2)*10000/96`).
Transcription slip; `tables.h`'s own comment already named the correct
formula.

[source,c]
----
// before (tables.c:62-68) -- no log10, never goes below 1000
double x = (double)i / 200.0;
double v = 1000.0 + (x * x * 10000.0) * (1.0 / 96.0);

// after -- SPEC.adoc T.4, using rt_log10 (this file's own convention for
// every other log-based table here: g_table_vel, g_table_lin and
// g_table_companding all already call rt_log10, not libm)
double x = (double)i * 0.004999999888241291; // 1/200 as float32, 0x11d04
double v = rt_log10(x * x) * 10000.0 * 0.010416666977107525 + 1000.0; // 1/96 float32, 0x11cfc
----

**The float32 constants are load-bearing, not cosmetic.** The `.rdata`
pool stores `1/200` as float32 `0.004999999888241291`, not the exact
decimal `0.005`. Confirmed by direct computation of both variants against
`src/unit.c`'s golden 201-entry dump:

[options="header"]
|===
| variant | mismatches vs. golden (201 entries) | t[1] | t[200]
| double `1.0/200.0`, `1.0/96.0` | 1 (index 200 only) | 520 | **1000** (wrong)
| float32 constants as stored | **0** | 520 | **999**
|===

`rt_log10` (this project's from-scratch log10, already used by three other
tables in this same file) reproduces the same zero-mismatch result as
libm's `log10` at every one of the 201 entries -- confirmed by direct
substitution, not assumed; no accuracy gap at this table's resolution.

**Correcting the worklist's own error:** `artifacts/INVESTIGATE.adoc`
`[#i1]` states "SPEC's table is monotonic-in-dB from `t[1] = -22`" -- that
figure appears nowhere in SPEC.adoc's own T.4 dump or in `unit.c`'s golden
array; both agree `t[1] = 520`. Not propagated here.

=== What reads Table C

Exhaustive whole-image search (every hex literal in the disassembly, plus
a raw-byte scan of the whole 56,576-byte file, independently) finds exactly
three references to the `0x1a9d8..0x1ab6a` range: the builder's zero-init
and store loop (already known), and one read, `movswl
0x1a9d8(,%eax,2),%eax` at `0x18b15`, inside the attack branch of the
shared envelope evaluator `EvalEnvelope` (`0x18a7a`):

[source,c]
----
// EvalEnvelope(seg, now), attack branch                        // 0x18ad3-0x18b24
ratio_permille = elapsed * 1000 / seg->attackDur;   // 64-bit mul+div  // 0x1049c, 0x10490
idx            = ratio_permille / 5;                // 64-bit div      // 0x10490
level          = g_table_envshape[idx];             // sign-extended int16 // 0x18b15
return level;                                        // 0..1000 on the 96 dB scale
----

This matches SPEC.adoc S3.4.2's evaluator table row verbatim (`attack |
TableC[(elapsed*1000/attackDuration)/5] | 0x18ad3-0x18b24`). **SPEC.adoc
already states this correctly; no SPEC.adoc change.** The table is not
dead in the original -- only in this project's engine, where nothing reads
`g_table_envshape` still (unchanged by this pass; see below).

=== The question behind the bug: is the port's attack shape wrong?

`artifacts/INVESTIGATE.adoc`'s `[#i1]` speculates the port's attack might
be shape-wrong somewhere `voice.c`'s `ENV_SPAN_DB` "papers over." **Checked
and rejected.** Table C's map, `L(ratio) = 1000*(1+20*log10(ratio)/96)`,
inverts to `ratio = 10^(((L/1000)-1)*96/20)` -- a level advancing linearly
in amplitude and a level read out of Table C describe the *same* curve;
Table C is a representation change (amplitude -> permille-of-96dB), not a
shape change. `voice.c`'s attack (`env_level += env_attack_step`,
`env_attack_step = 1/(attack_s*RENDER_RATE)`) already advances linearly in
amplitude, in double precision -- exactly the SPEC-documented pre-image of
Table C's map. `voice.c` also already computes Table C's own formula
analytically elsewhere (`start_release`'s fast-release path: `1.0 +
20*rt_log10(env_level)/ENV_SPAN_DB`), so the formula itself is not
unfamiliar to this file.

**Confirmed independently, not just algebraically:** a scratch program
stepped `voice.c`'s continuous ramp and a Table-C-quantized readout
(`10^(((TableC[idx]/1000)-1)*96/20)`) sample-by-sample across six attack
durations (3 ms .. 3 s) and measured the gap between them at every sample:

[options="header"]
|===
| attack duration | samples | max \|amplitude diff\| | rms diff
| 3 ms | 66 | 0.0117 | 0.0051
| 10 ms | 221 | 0.0162 | 0.0072
| 50 ms | 1103 | 0.0144 | 0.0063
| 200 ms | 4410 | 0.0146 | 0.0061
| 1 s | 22050 | 0.0146 | 0.0061
| 3 s | 66150 | 0.0147 | 0.0061
|===

The gap is bounded (~0.015 peak, ~0.006 rms) and does not grow with
duration -- the signature of a fixed 201-step *quantization* gap, not a
diverging shape error. **`ENV_SPAN_DB = 96.0` is correct and is not
papering over a shape bug; the only real difference is quantization**: the
original's attack level can take at most 201 distinct values (`int16`,
`trunc()`-quantized, indexed by `permille/5`), `voice.c`'s is continuous.

=== A/B: does the quantization matter?

Implemented behind `ENV_ATTACK_TABLE_C` (default `0`, the same
`-D`-overridable pattern this file already uses for
`EG2_LINEAR_SEGMENTS`/`RELEASE_RATE_MULT`/etc.) in `src/engine/voice.c`:
when `1`, `voice_step_envelope`'s `ENV_ATTACK` case tracks elapsed/total
samples and reads `g_table_envshape` through the inverse-dB formula above
instead of the linear increment; it lands on exactly `1.0` once
`elapsed>=attackSamples` (matching `0x18ac0-0x18ad1`'s own transition,
which leaves the attack branch -- and Table C -- entirely at that point,
rather than ever reading the table at `ratio==1.0`, where `t[200]=999`
would otherwise leave a small permanent gain deficiency going into decay).

`.venv/bin/python artifacts/score.py` (default OFF) vs. a second binary
built `-DENV_ATTACK_TABLE_C=1` via `MSGS_RENDER=...`, both against the
`after_i3.txt` baseline (pan law, item45, landed since `base.txt`):

[options="header"]
|===
| item | r (OFF) | r (ON) | residual (OFF) | residual (ON) | Δresidual
| 04_envelope (targeted) | 0.983 | 0.983 | -40.63 | -40.58 | +0.05 (worse)
| 32_ramp_shape (targeted) | 0.902 | 0.902 | -46.94 | -46.94 | 0.00
| 41_sustain_decay_curve (targeted) | 0.995 | 0.995 | -51.01 | -50.90 | +0.11 (worse)
| 44_release_shape (targeted) | 0.976 | 0.976 | -43.59 | -43.44 | +0.15 (worse)
| MEAN (68 items) | 0.928 | 0.928 | -31.53 | -31.52 | +0.01 (worse)
|===

**None of the four targeted envelope items improved**; `r` is unchanged to
three decimals on all four, and the residual moves slightly worse (less
negative) on three of the four and is unchanged on the fourth
(32_ramp_shape). The mean also moves marginally worse. Per this project's
own ship rule (targeted items must improve AND the mean must not regress),
**the quantized variant is not shipped**; `ENV_ATTACK_TABLE_C` stays `0` by
default. This is not attributed to a shape difference (the previous
section already establishes both curves converge to the same bounded gap
regardless of duration) -- most likely `render.c`'s own segment-endpoint
gain interpolation (`GAIN_SEGMENT_FRAMES`-bounded ramps, independent of
this item) already smooths over sub-segment detail for both variants
alike, leaving only the small extra residual the log curve's
discretization adds over a purely linear one. Left for a future pass, not
chased further here.

=== Corpus expectation confirmed, not assumed

The `tables.c` fix alone is **inert on the corpus**: nothing in
`src/engine` reads `g_table_envshape` by default (the guarded `voice.c`
variant above is opt-in, default OFF). Full 68-item `artifacts/score.py`
run after the `tables.c` fix, diffed byte-for-byte against `after_i3.txt`:
`diff` exit code 0, zero output -- every `r`/residual/dead figure
unchanged. Confirmed by running the diff, not inferred from "nothing reads
it".

'''

'''

== 2026-08-01 — Pan law now interpolates SPEC's own 9-anchor measured table directly; the `g_table_lin`-pivot law let the near channel rise up to +3.04 dB above unity — item45, SHIPPED per SPEC, corpus-wide mean regresses marginally (see Corpus expectation)

**Status: SHIPPED.** `src/engine/voice.c`'s `voice_update_gain()` no longer
computes either pan channel from the disassembly-derived `g_table_lin`
table (the law SPEC_LOG.adoc xref:entry7[Entry 7] shipped); it interpolates
SPEC.adoc xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-6[S3.6]'s own nine measured CC10 anchors
directly. This supersedes xref:entry7[Entry 7]'s shipped code — Entry 7's

precedent).

**Path:** `artifacts/INVESTIGATE.adoc` `[#i3]`; SPEC.adoc
xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-6[S3.6] (the 9-anchor `[M: probe 25]` table — already

(comment block + `voice_update_gain`).

**Tests:** 175, 178, 179, 180 (`src/unit.c`, S3.6 block — renumbered from

(direction-only checks) were already green and stay green. 173 (S3.5/
